End of contest. No one wants it, no one gets it. Moderators, if you can, please delete this thread, unless someone really wants this contest.


Alright. My first contest, so bear with me here. First off, I am in need of help. I need judges, and I need people who can (if possible) update the list when I am not here. If you wish to be a judge, PM me, do not reply in this thread, please.

Judges:
capt_weasle
ultima888
kirby

Backup Judges:
greengooguy49

Rules:
~ For now, 2 entries per person. You may delete a previous entry and replace it with a new one, but please don't do it multiple times.
~ You may have the image as small or as big as you'd like.
~ DO NOT make anything that is porn-related. No gang related symbles, or anything that others may find offensive. No images that make fun of/harrass others or their religion, culture, etc.
~ You may NOT use N-art maker, whatso-ever.
~ No plaigerism (sp?). If it's not yours, don't use it. I don't care if you have the member's permission or not, we want original work.
~ No submitting old work. Make up something new.
~ After making a map, please post a link to it in this thread. I'm too unorganized to look it up on NUMA myself. If I happen to see it roaming the site, I'll add it to the list, but please put a link here, because if you do not, there is a chance it will be missed.
~ If you can, put a link to this contest in your map discription. It is not recquired, but I'll appreciate it if you do.

Judging:
Each entry will recieve a vote from 1-10, 1 being crappy, and 10 being totally freakin awesome.

For now, there is no deadline, so use that time while I'm busy with school before I post an official date. There might be a prize, but for now I can't think of one.

Be creative and have fun.

ENTRIES:

#153925 2 2006-03-12T02:17:16Z capt_weasle
This post will be seperate from the orginal to save space and to seperate the map links from the storyline. Comments on the story so far are greatly appreciated.

FrameShift
~00~

00-00 ~ Wreckage
Tie straight. Suit buttoned. Wrinkle in the left corner. Not anymore, smoothed now. Good. Damien Parker walked out of the bathroom, a smug drin drawn out over his lips. Today was going to be a good day. Job promo, date saturday, everything was planned out for the week, life was swell. He walked out of his 2 story surburban home, locked it up, and set off to his Cadillac XLR-V. Key turned in ignition. The car hummed to life. He slammed on the pedal and with a squeal the car jolted onto the road. No point in going slow today. His promotion awaited.
Traffic was slow. He hated back-ups. He lowered the roof and stuck his head out over the window. Nothing seemed wrong, no accidents. This was strange. Wait, what was that? A whisper in the air drifted across the freeway, quickly growing into a loud hum. What? Suddenly a loud bang, and a car was in the air. In the air? That couldn't be right. The car slammed into the ground, crushing another smaller vehicle. The driver of the smaller car was racing to get away from the wreck. Two more cars sprang up, landing farther away, one in a ditch, the other on top of a tow truck. Screams of panic rose in the street. Most were already out of their cars and running for cover. No way was he going to -- a large truck obscured his vision, and he instintively jumped out of the car, just as the truck slammed into his car. For once not wearing a seatbelt actually saved his life. That car was brand new. He cursed under his breath and jogged across to the edge of the freeway to see what was going on. A large....large....stop sign!? What the... Another soft hum, and suddenly a beam shot out of the "stop sign", searing a semi-truck in half. What in the world was going on? He had to get out of here. He ran for the nearest building. But it wasn't a building. It was rubble with a door. His eyes widened. He looked out towards downtown. There were dozens more of the octagonal shaped objects shooting bright red beams, melting anything in their path. He looked back at the ruined building. Another hum, this time directly behind him. He reached for the doorknob and entered what was left of the building as fast as he could. This would have to do. Little shelter from those aliens, or whatever they were, was better than no shelter.

00-01 ~ 3/4 of a Dream

He burst into the building just as the red "laser" ripped through a side wall. He quickly glanced at his surroundings. Stairs, he needed to get to the stairs. He ran, as fast as he could. Another room. More....more....octagons. This time they were blue. Great, what do these do? A few in the far back had antenneas on them. Odd. Wait, what are those red dots? Are those.....are those mines!? No, they coulnd't be. Mines did not float. Great. And, yellow...no, gold, cubes. Oookaayy. This was dumb. What in the - he stopped running and doubled over. Sickness swept over his body. What? Ooooh. He felt like....he was going to die. Suddenly there was a craving for the gold cubes. Maybe they'll help, who knows anymore? He ran out into the room.

(Game plays as in level 00-00)

Damien stopped. Again. He felt. As. if. he could 't mo.
He passed out. A dream. Black and white. Now gray, red, and gold. Clearer now. Gold dots, red dots. Avoid red, go for gold. Get blue rectangle. The voice screamed at him. GO! Get to the door! NOW! But, it was a dream, wasn't it? A dre - GO! And so he went. Dream or no dream, the voice seemed real enough. He drifted off to get to some unkown door.

#157927 13 2006-03-25T23:26:36Z capt_weasle
Stop complaining. Look, there are several people who would want to be in your position. You're not in the top tops anymore, so what? There will always be new people climbing the ranks, but ya know what? They won't be there for long. There's just too much talent in this world. Also, in agreement with what blue_tetris said, if you don't show up here, it's not likely that you'll be mentioned or whatever frequently. People don't come, they aren't remembered (with a few exceptions). Memories fade, so if you frequent the forum, then you won't have much to complain about. And you were included in the Legacy Project, weren't you? I will never be able to come up with maps like that. Ever. I'm not recognised for doing anything great. It's likely that I never will, either. Hardly anyone plays my maps, and that was since I started making them. The only maps of mine that are really rated are some of my N-arts, which no one likes n art anymore anyway. Also, as many people before me have said, ratings are just that, ratings. Nothing more, nothing less. Sure, just about everyone cares about them. It's how it's always been, and it probably won't change. That's why snipers are such a big deal to most people. I think that comments are usually better than ratings, because people can actually say how they felt about the maps, not just a 1 a 2 or a 5. Heck, I care about my ratings. I like the feeling that people are enjoying my maps, and I'm sad if people rate them down for no reason. And if they have a reason, it should point out flaws in the map, and that only helps me see what I can do to improve them. And, yes, I complain too. And why is that? Because I am human. And unless anyone here isn't, everyone complains. So I'm not trying to bash on you or anything, I'm just saying that you should keep making maps and doign stuff on the forums. People still will enjoy your maps, whether they rate or not, and I'm sure there are plenty of others who look up to you (see whaleshow's comment). Don't quit now.

#8796 1 2006-05-18T22:06:00Z capt_weasle
I didn't know where to put this, so I decided here would be best. Enjoy the story, it will be added on to in the near future, so don't worry . Well, here you are! Enjoy!

--- --- --- ---

There was a time when everything was peaceful. Not peace as we may know it, but a settle, relaxed tone about life. Everyone had their differences, always have and they always will. No force on heaven or earth could change that. But this was a time when there wasn't a race for power, money, or survival. No one was at war. However, plain common sense can point out that peace does not hold for very long. Hunger for gold, a hunger for death. There were some who did not agree with the way of N. They advocated for a new system. Unfortunately, this was a system of slavery. It did not settle well with the devout believers of N, for they were the ones who were suggested to be sold into slavery. Soon a schism broke out between races. Some went one way, others on their own. Everyone has their differences.

The Drones were never an aggressive race. They were not created to serve, nor created to destroy. Like everyone else, they had their free will. And it was their choice to follow the dark path. A choice that once followed, offered no way back. A one way platform, one might say. A leader arose some amount of time before the schism, declaring that a change in government be made. A law that should be passed, he explained, to contain Man's "oppression". Slavery the counselors called it. But this political leader would not accept the term. He thought it of a way to stop prejudice. Man's thirst for the gold was becoming too great. What he did not understand was that Man needed it for the survival of their race. Drones had not a need for this gold, the counselors pleaded. But he would not have it.

This was around two years before the break of government. During those years the Drone's political leader conducted speeches to gain his people's trust and confidence. He told his followers about his 'path of freedom'. He made every attempt, reasonable or not, to grasp their attention. And after two years, most had chosen his path. Yet some refused, joining by Man's side to stand against the leader. Man and Drone, side by side. Something rarely seen these days. One more year and war broke out.

The High counselors would not stand for it. They wreaked their powers on all who fought. New rules had been laid down. Drones were to be striped of their freedom. Some were granted the ability to seek, but it was highly unreliable. The others were programmed to a specific path, and were to stay there for as long as they lived. There were divisions among them as well. Some assigned to guard, with chain guns loaded at their sides. Others could dispatch lasers to attack oncoming threats. And the ones who chose not to cooperate were fully relieved of their abilities. They were to crawl on the floors, nothing more, and nothing less, for all eternity.

Man had his own privileges revoked as well. Life cut short, only their thirst of gold could keep them alive. The High counselors removed all but a few caches of gold. They created a system of mazes to hide the gold. For years they had not been found. Man's numbers drastically fell, almost to the point of their extinction. But two unlikely travelers stumbled upon them deep in a forest on the edge of the land. Soon everyone knew about them, and there was a mass exodus to these mazes. The counselors knew they would find the mazes someday, so they laced them with traps and mines.

The Drone leader soon got word of the mazes, and he ordered all of his followers to them. And from this day on, they guard the hoards of gold stowed away in the mazes. The surviving race of Man had been granted a special metabolism to help them get past the Drones, as the High counselors thought it fair, as long as the Drones were still a threat. This new metabolism was fueled by the gold, but could run out.

And to this present day, Man, and those who followed the way of N, were called Ninjas. Several Tribes of Ninjas were formed, and were all separate colors, yet all were banded together into one covenant, devoted to getting all the gold. They hope to seek a way to return the peace between themselves and their opponents. And this is their story...
#178013 2 2006-05-20T05:27:10Z capt_weasle
Saturday. Worlds gotta end sometime. Walter Johnson strolled into the grand hotel, a refuge for those who weren't doing the "I told you so's" down in the heavy parts of the city. The freaks. I guess they were right, though. Heh, it just goes to show, if you spend every day of your life out in the streets wavin' and yellin' at everyone, "The End is Near!", you have to be right some day. Kinda like if someone tells you to pick a number between one and ten. If you keep saying 1, chances are you'll be right at least once. But the end of the world was just that. Once. Seemed everyone was crazy today. Livin' up life to the fullest. But not him, no not Walter. No no no, he was going to live it like he did any other day. They were all going to die anyway. No point in doing anything grand. Not unless you can live through it and get something good out of it. Unless the scientists were wrong, and everyone realised that they were'nt going to die after giving away everything. Now that would be fun.

He walked up to the counter, and tapped his palm against the shiny brass bell on the counter three times. "Service around here?".

#179593 5 2006-05-23T22:04:45Z capt_weasle
Episode 1:

Dark clouds smeared the gray sky above. Fires streaked across the fields, no sign of plant life for miles around. The battle grounds were an entirely different scene than the mazes just east of the field. Here was different. Bodies lay on the ground, breath ripped out of their souls. Electrical parts, tossed about like a childs toys. You didnt see this in the mazes. There it was more orderly. Everything deceased was cleaned up by unknown forces, making an eerily clean haunted house of tunnels. Yet on the barren battle grounds far away from any cities or establishments, there was a feeling of decay in the air. Such a strong sense of death that you could almost taste it.

South of the grounds revealed camps. A couple hundred, at least. Women set about their daily chores, cooking and cleaning. Children played out in the open, laughing without any knowledge of what was really going on. Any disabled men that could not hunt or gather stayed behind, aiding in the development of homes or attending medical tents to treat the ill or injured.

Most of the warriors went off to fight if they could find it. Although they were unable to use any kind of force gathering gold in the labyrinths, while fighting they could muster every ounce of energy and weaponry to hold off any enemy that approached. But for now it was quiet, not a Drone in sight.

A low campfire lit up the darkening sky. Three figures huddled around it, attempting to warm their frigid bodies. Night was approaching, and night meant enemies. The small scout group could not withstand an ambush. They would have to head back to the camps soon, or risk being open targets. One of them shivered violently, a wisp of cold air disappearing into the air.

We should pack up. Jack looked up, eyes bloodshot.

Not yet, we have to wait until a half hour after sunset. Then we go. Jack looked to Russell, who had been pleading to go back for the past 45 minutes. Both of them knew that they were prohibited from going anywhere until their shift was over. Unless there was some sort of attack or injury, they were to stay put.

Im with Jack, we cant leave. We only have about an hour left, anyway. Sarah was the youngest in the group, and one of the scarce women that served their military. Young, but tough. The scout assembly before us didnt report anything, and were not expecting another wave of Drones for at least another few days.

Russell scowled, still advocating that they leave. I dont like this silence. Jack laid down on his pack and glanced at him, one eye open.

Come on, Russ. You know that its always like this at sunset. Always has, and its not bound to change. The trio was just recently assigned the shift at dusk, and it was only their second night out. Sarah stood, looking at the moon just coming out behind the deep cloud layer. A shaft of light spread out over the valley, shoving away the darkness. She looked back at them.

Full moon tonight, thats a relief. At least we can get some light on the way back home. Besides, we... but something made her stop. A worried look hit both Jack and Russell in unison. Something was wrong. Both of them got to their knees and then slowly advanced to their feet. A glimmer about a mile out into the field. Sarah stepped back.

Uh, guys, I think we might want to get out of here He knew in an instant what it was. Scout Drone. In a matter of minutes it would spot them if they didnt move. Now. He reached down and grabbed his backpack. He started down the hill, his internal compass telling him he was going north towards the camp.

Sarah was just about to start after him, but Russ grabbed her arm. What about the fire? We ought to put it out! But she was already pulling him down the hill. No time! was all she could manage before an alarm broke the silence that overshadowed the valley. This was not good. All three of them knew what was happening. The field for miles around would be flooded with Drones in a matter of hours.

They had to get back to camp.

#183229 14 2006-06-01T22:12:20Z capt_weasle
Not a big update today, but I'm not really in a mood to write a lot today. Enjoy though!

-------

Episode 2:

"Run. Run back to your camps, but deep down inside you know it's not worth it." Death lingered in the air. Fear and agony so stagnant you could reach out and touch it. "Fools such as you will be dealt with. You death will be a slow and excruciating one." The caverns lay dormant since they were created. No life, as none could be supported in the desolate place. Yet although nothing was present, it was there. Spirits drifting in the fog, faceless and emotionless. Screams of thousands of innocents echoed in the nights, rays of light breaching holes in the ceiling illuminating the dead. "The wager of sin is death. The guilty shall receive their rightful place in Hell, and you are guilty."

* * *

Sarah reached the camp first, closely followed by Jack and Russ. They rushed into the General's tent, breathing hard. General Bradley Saunders stood from his chair, gleaming into their eyes. "What is it, soldiers?" Jack drew in a deep breath and explained.

"Scout drone, 2 miles to the south. They'll be here shortly, we must act quickly!"

Sarah intervened, "Sir, if we don't assemble the rest of the troops now, we'll get caught unprepared." General Saunders crossed the tent to a table nearby and snatched up a radio. The megaphones outside screeched then reduced to a soft crackle.

"Attention! This is not a drill, I repeat, this is not a drill! All able-bodied soldiers report to the front of the camps." He set down the radio and flicked a switch. Sirens screamed out of the horns, signaling an attack.

Jack, Russ, and Sarah ran outside to the front field where every other soldier gathered. In their hands were spears, rocks, or anything else they could find for defense. Jack snatched up his own spear and trotted to the beginning of the formation. Russ and Sarah would be spread out among the other sections.

Suddenly everyone went quiet. A low humming noise broke out across the valley, slowly growing into an ear shattering cry. Lines of dark blue appeared over a hill just over a half mile away. Drones.

It was going to be a long night.

Colossus[/u]

Story: It's the year 3010. A new planet has discovered lingering on the outside of our solar system. Named after its founder Jacob Colossi, the planet has recently been colonized by eager immigrants hoping to escape the crowded Earth.

RULES:

- Only one country per person, unless you have attacked and conquered another country.
- You may not create your country with any more than what is given to you at the start. IE: You may NOT start off with more than the allowed number of citizens.
- For now you may only play ONE leader. No making two leaders to gain more territory.
- You are allowed to name your country and choose where you want your Capital within your own country.

Starting a Country:

- Once I have 10 people interested, the game will begin.
- AFTER the game begins, you will choose a number 2-17 (The numbers will be chosen randomly so there are no arguments over who gets the bigger land).
- First come, first serve when choosing numbers.
- Once you have been assigned a country, you will need to fill out this form:

Country name: Try to be somewhat realistic here, no really stupid names (IE: Noobl4nd).
Leader Name: Be creative. Something other than your user name would be appreciated.
Flag: Your flag will consist of a field (background) and design/symbol. (Ex: Black field with skull).
Leadership: Democracy, Communism, Dictatorship, etc. No made up leaderships please.
Religion: Choose from the religions that already exist. If your country has a mixed religion, then just put N/A.
Main Race: No aliens. Anything that we see here on earth is acceptable.
Population: EVERYONE will start out with 500 citizens.
Military Force: You may develop a militia after you have 1,000 citizens. You budget will affect how many soldiers you are given.
Budget: Everyone starts with an amount of $1,000,000.

rincewind sw

Country name: Jyrk'on

Budget: J$ 5,000,000 (Equivalent to $1,000,000


Country Descriptions

New Colossi (1):

Right below the equator, this country has a very pleasant climate. Lake Bisanari provides an adequate supply of fresh water to towns and cities. Mostly flat land, the nutrition-rich soil is perfect for farming. However, during the late winter months storms that approach the east coast are disastrous. The low land is easily flooded, which proves to be a major problem in developing any establishments.

Country 2 (Unnamed):

This is a country of opposites. The south end is very hot and humid. This dry climate makes for poor farming, except around the north portion of Lake Bisanari. The north end of the country, however, is extremely cold and icy. Life is hardly sustainable up there, and only the people with the knowledge and skills to live in such a place live here.

Cistercia (3):

Cold in the winter, hot in the summer, this island country is a very desirable place. Fishing is an important source of income here, but Cistercia could not survive without it's attraction of foreigners. Long, smooth beaches and large waves make tourism a valuable resource. Yet during rainy months, unprotected land is at risk of hurricanes.

Exendergarsen (4) and Alagaësia (16):

The Gerimbaudi (Gare-im-body) desert covers most of the north, dry and merciless sand dunes dominating most of the land. Fortunately, Lake Adomi provides a rich oasis for which to grow crops and collect fresh water. In the middle region, the Meritian (Mare-ish-in) Highlands, the Sade (Sa-day) river flows in at almost 18 miles wide and 134 miles long, this is the largest river in Colossi. Scientists classify it as a long lake, even though it narrows into a 3 mile wide river. Off to the south, the Aranian Peninsula creates a small bay for incoming ships. There the climate is rather warm, generally humid. Small, natural beaches laden the coastline near the tip of the peninsula, making it a welcome site in the eyes of weary travelers. But they stay warm for only a short couple months, as they make way to the cold winters ahead.

Austra-Flaica (10) and countries Xarosia (5), Dawnduskii (7), 8, and 9 (Unnamed):

This mountainous region is location to 5 separate countries. The Remington Mountains are the center of all the political boundaries, also known as the 4 corners (which, coincidentally, also has a place in the United States on Earth). The largest east country holds the majority of the fresh water available to the rest of the countries in the continent. This is a big source of income for that country, as the others need to either buy from them, import fresh water from neighboring continents (very expensive), or convert the salt water from the Ricarde Ocean into drinkable fresh water (even more expensive).

Nordaugh (11), Barcelonia (12), and country 13 (Unnamed):

With the help of satellite photos, geologists were able to conclude that these 3 countries used to be one, large one. Together, the three countries create what used to be known as Angosnia. The middle country is covered in a tropical rainforest. This forest provides many materials, such as medicines and food. The northern country is somewhat similar to Europe, a foggy and rainy climate. Harsh, cold winters yield to humid and rainy summers. The southernmost country is cold and dry. A barren tundra, life is hard to maintain, but isn't impossible to support a few cities.


Arelius (6) and country 17 (Unnamed):

Made of all flat land, these two countries contain all of the perfect ingredients to farm. Rich soils make demand for the freshest crops extremely high. These two countries alone provide over 35% of all crops produced in Colossi. The rainy seasons are short yet hard, and extra care and money are put in into making sure the fields don't drown.

Raskousia (14) and country 17 (Unnamed):

Two very similar countries, they bear extreme resemblance to our own north and south poles here on Earth. Long days in the summer and long nights in the winter (North and South poles vary), the rugged landscapes are home to a frozen wasteland. The largest icebergs anyone has ever seen, shadowing even the massive Superberg off the coast of Antarctica. But people find a way to live here; doing whatever needs to be done in order to stay warm. You wouldn't want to get stuck in a blizzard here.


Natural Resources[/u]

New Colossi: Gold, iron, natural gas, copper, and nickel
Country 2: Diamond, nickel, copper, iron, and bauxite
Cistercia: Natural gas, gold, iron, bauxite, and steel
Exendergarsen: Steel, iron, nickel, and oil reserves
Xarosi: Holds part of the freshwater lake, diamond, and copper
Arelius: Rich soils provide good farming, coal, copper
Dawnduskii: Holds almost all of the freshwater supply for that region, diamond, steel, nickel
Country 8: Diamond, nickel, bauxite, uranium, and oil
Country 9: Gold, uranium, iron, and steel
Austra-Flaica: Copper, iron, nickel, chrome
Nordaugh: Oil, natural gas, copper
Barcelonia: Gold, uranium, and other resources provided from the Rainforest
Solta: Oil, nickel, chrome
Raskousia: Diamond, copper, bauxite, chlorine
Country 15: Gold, copper, steel, sodium
Alagaesia: Bauxite, magnesium, sulfur
Country 17: Rich soil provides good farming, coal, copper

#189718 15 2006-06-15T03:21:05Z capt_weasle
How to Play[/u]

- To start off, you need to develop your capital city. Build hospitals, banks, whatever you think will help you start off. A lot of these buildings will be important later on in the game (IE - Hospitals to provide health care in battles)
- Houses are a must. Peeps need a pad to crash, dawg.
- You may build a shipyard if you wish. These are definitely important, especially if you have recourses to export, or import goods from other countries, as well as being able to send out soldiers in war.
- Airports can develop as the game progresses. These are for bringing in people and transportation to/from war zones (again).
- Some items you buy can provide an income. Casinos, hotels, and other type of tourist-related activities can help you earn some cash.
- Taxes will be taken every now and then. I'll total up how much you've spent and decide how much more you will owe.
- Prices on buildings will go up as the game progresses.
- Please DO NOT buy all of the large buildings as of now.
- Every country has natural resources. Whether it be gold or oil, every place has something, so don't complain.
- As moderator god of this game, I reserve the right to manipulate you an any way I feel needed (but I'm pretty nice). If I feel as though you aren't keeping your community happy, or aren't paying up on anything, I will allow your citizens to riot. This will deduct your budget for damage costs.
- If you have natural resources such as gold or iron, you need to buy MINES and MINERAL REFINERIES before you can export your goods. If you have natural resources such as oil, you need to buy OIL RIGS (either land or sea) and OIL REFINERIES before you can export those goods.

Expenses

These will be posted after everyone has submitted a letter regarding the shuttle.

--::FIRST ASSIGNMENT::--[/u]

ALL countries MUST participate!

A cargo shuttle transporting 5,000 eager citizens has just arrived from Earth. They are interested to know which country they will be settling into. It is your job to convince them to move into your country. You must write at least a paragraph explaining why you are the best candidate in supporting these people. I will read through them, and the most convincing will earn the most people, and then the next person will receive a few less, and so on and so forth. Everyone will receive some citizens, but some will get more than others. In addition to earning the most people, the best paragraph will receive $10,000. The runner up will get 7,000, and continue down the line. I will evaluate and choose the winner once every country has submitted their letter. Let's get it going!

#200803 2 2006-07-10T01:28:58Z capt_weasle
Alright, listen up. Here's what it's all about. Actually, here's a link first - link!

Not really an awesome map, but hey, we're doing our best...sorta. Anyway, you are given a map (click the link). Each map has at least 2 doors. I will post a 'story' here that goes to each map, and explains the situation, and what each door leads to. Every door in the map will be either for good or evil. It's up to you to choose which one to go through. Some doors will be harder to get to, others will be easier, that all depends on what you are after. Sometimes there will be rewards for going through one of the doors. Sometimes there will be consequences. But we will get to that later.

In this first map neither door affects you. You just awoke, so now you need to decide where to go first. This is kind of like the Epic, only more involved.

If you have any questions feel free to ask in this thread.

Day One[/u]

Where am I? This place doesnt look familiar. And.is that, blood? Why is my head bleeding? What.this lookslike.a forest? Who, who am I? I dont remember coming here. Where was I last? Running, fromcant remember. Where am I supposed to go?

- There are two paths. Which one do you take? -

New levels! Alright, in the first map here, there are the two doors. The door on the far right leads to this map here, and the door on the far left leads to this map here. In the first level, you chose a door, so play the map that that door leads to.

Day Two

- (If you chose the door to the left)
You seem to have chosen the right door. At least now you are out of the Moss Forest. Just out of the forest is a rocky outcrop. You can either go to there, but the other door is higher in the trees of the forest. Must lead to the canopy. Where to go?

> Go to the Canopy
or
> Go to the rocks

- (If you chose the door to the right)
Great. More forest. Oh well. Looks almost the same here. Two doors. One leads even deeper into the forest...and the other...um....leads into the ground. Okay...

> Go deeper into the forest
or
> Go underground.

#203884 2 2006-07-17T23:13:43Z capt_weasle


How to Play Part I - Expenses[/u]
- To start off, you need to develop your capital city. Build hospitals, banks, whatever you think will help you start off. A lot of these buildings will be important later on in the game (IE - Hospitals to provide health care in battles)
- Houses are a must. Peeps need a pad to crash, dawg.
- You may build a shipyard if you wish. These are definitely important, especially if you have recourses to export, or import goods from other countries, as well as being able to send out soldiers in war.
- Airports can develop as the game progresses. These are for bringing in people and transportation to/from war zones (again).
- Some items you buy can provide an income. Casinos, hotels, and other type of tourist-related activities can help you earn some cash.
- Taxes will be taken every now and then. I'll total up how much you've spent and decide how much more you will owe.
- Prices on buildings will go up as the game progresses.
- Please DO NOT buy all of the large buildings as of now.
- Every country has natural resources. Whether it is gold or oil, every place has something, so don't complain.
- As moderator god of this game, I reserve the right to manipulate you in any way I feel needed (but I'm pretty nice). If I feel as though you aren't keeping your community happy, or aren't paying up on anything, I will allow your citizens to riot. This will deduct your budget for damage costs.
- If you have natural resources such as gold or iron, you need to buy MINES and MINERAL REFINERIES before you can export your goods. If you have natural resources such as oil, you need to buy OIL RIGS (either land or sea) and OIL REFINERIES before you can export those goods.
- If you own a WASTE-TO-ENERGY plant, you do not require a LANDFILL region.
- You may not build ships until after you own a SHIP YARD. After that, each ship costs money. (Either wood or metal).

#10942 1 2006-08-28T22:38:00Z capt_weasle
Before I start, let me first say some things.
1) This is not discussing the book. I am here to explain some ideas from the book. Although I don't care if you use the book as a preference.
2) Please do not come in here and start saying, "That book sucked. I hate summer assignments", etc.
3) If you've not read the book, read what I've written and discuss on that.
EDIT: Also, although you may inform me if I've written something wrong, this isn't a grammar check. I want you to reflect on my ideas and give me a response.

Anyways. I had to read half of "Sophie's World" over my summer (and am currently working on some other summer hw). Now I have to choose one philosophy from the book to agree with, and one to disagree with. I wanted to hear what you guys have to say on the subject. Here is my reasoning on what philosphy I disagree with. I encourage you to make comments on what I've written, good or bad. I'd like to know your opinion.

Disagreement:
Socrates once said that, He who knows what is good will do good (Gaarder 69). Although I do agree with this statement, I believe this is not always the case. When we do wrong it is because we dont know any better (Gaarder 69). Usually these people are young, those who have not yet learned what is acceptable in society and what is not. Among older men and women, it is sometimes the case that a person may have a mental disorder that renders them unable to distinguish right versus wrong. Yet in most cases these people can still make their own decisions based on common sense. So, if you know what youre doing is good, youll follow through with it. If you know what youre doing or about to do is wrong, you wont.

If doing the right thing will make us happy, who would do the wrong thing? Therefore, he who knows what is right will do right. Because why would anybody choose to be unhappy? (Gaarder 69). Here is where I begin to disagree with Socrates. Almost everyday we see and hear stories in the news about robberies and murder. Now Id assume that most everyone in the world knows that killing and stealing is wrong, so why do we do it? The most important reason is because of self-gain. Today, society teaches us that he who dies with the most toys wins. If you dont have the looks, the money, or the stuff, you wont succeed in life. Unfortunately, we all feel this at some point in life. This is why there are some people who will resort to killing, stealing, lying, and cheating just to get one step up the ladder. But we know it is wrong. Almost without realizing it, we are choosing to be unhappy.

Can we realize that we are making ourselves lead an unhappy way of life? Are they aware that these things are not rightor fair, if you prefer? (Gaarder 69). I believe the answer is yes. Imagine a small child, staring hungrily at a full cookie jar, waiting for the chance to plunge his hands into it and snatch away some treat. His mother reminds him he is not to eat a cookie before dinner. Im sure you can guess what happens. When her back is turned, the child discreetly steals one of the cookies, well knowing that he is not supposed to. He eats it, and after only a few brief moments of pleasure, he is racked with guilt. Of course, his mother notes the crumbs circling around the childs mouth, and proceeds to punish him for his wrongful decision. As we get older, simply taking a cookie before dinner will not make us feel guilty. Why, most of us would take another, if given the chance. But there are other situations we may find ourselves in quite often. Another scenario: A teenager is given two weeks to prepare for a big English test. Of course, he is preoccupied with videogames and other activities. The day of the test arrives and our student finds himself in a deep pit. The test is set on his desk, and sweat begins to run down his face. His eyes inch over to his classmate, who is sitting next to him. He proceeds to cheat on the test. Given that this student does not cheat often, later he will feel guilty. He receives an A on the test, and neither his teacher nor his parents find out. In fact, his parents give him special privileges because of his outstanding work. The teenager goes on to live his life knowing that he cheated, and was praised for his work undeservingly. Now every time he sees a test, he is reminded of that day and continues to feel guilty, although it may not last forever. Once again, many students who cheat often will not feel an ounce of guilt, so this is when we see the bigger picture. Although we may not know from experience, we can assume that someone who has just committed murder or robbery will get caught in a guilt trap. It could haunt them day and night, and could lead to the beginning of a downward slope. So we realize our wrong decision, which often leads to guilt. But why do we continue leading this life? Many of us may feel as though we cannot be forgiven for the things we do, therefore they continue to do what is wrong. Others because they want to get further in life, and may not see what they are doing until years down the road, when they suffer the consequences of their actions and realize that what they did cannot be undone. So if he who knows what is good, will do good, does that mean that every single person who does something wrong doesnt know any better? I think that He who knows what is right, has the decision to do what it right, may be more accurate. Because even though you know the difference between right and wrong, you may still choose to do wrong simply because of how you choose to live your life.

#11007 1 2006-09-01T20:39:47Z capt_weasle
I sorta kinda got this idea from Trogdor007, but he got the idea from me, so it's really my idea. What is it, you ask? A digital art worm. For an example of what this is, look here. So, what are you here for? To help create this worm. What do I do? Here are the rules:

1. Start out with a template of 400x250. Plenty of room to make something.
2. You may contribute to this as much as you wish. Just add your name to the list. Just please do not go more than 1 time in a row.
3. The left edge of your section needs to match up with the previous entry.
4. No obscene images/text, please. Keep it safe.
5. Other than that, you can have anything on the section.
6. You can use any image program, even MS Paint.
7. Se7en is darker.
8. You can use either .png, .jpg, or .jpeg for your images.
9. If you wish to use an animation, 600kb is the limit.
10. Enjoy.

Once I get some people on the list, we can start. I don't really need many people to launch this, so we an get started fairly quickly.

This List is no longer here. Look here.

#239513 6 2006-10-11T05:01:24Z capt_weasle
Quote: Tsukatu, October 10, 2006 08:33 pm
This has actually happened to me sometimes when I was running Firefox under winblows.
Basically, the process doesn't close for some reason, and the new instance of firefox sees that there's already one running and tries to pass a New Window operation through the existing process instead of creating a new process on top of the old one (this is good to avoid bogging down your computer).
Press Ctrl+Alt+Del and click on the processes tab. There should be one called "firefox.exe" when you're running Firefox. If the situation you described above happens again, go to the Task Manager and end the "firefox.exe" process that is likely still running. Then try running Firefox again and it should be dandy.

That's happened to me on several occasions.

#11747 1 2006-10-15T04:09:13Z capt_weasle
Don't worry, this isn't for my vocab words. This is much better in my opinion. This is for my english class, though. We had to integrate phrases (prepositional, appositives, ect) into a story of our choosing. Bear with me on this one, it's a lot better than my last series. It's a fun read. Short, too.

- - - - -

Sorry Mr. Turner, there aint nuthin wrong with yer sink, Donald looked up from his squatting position under the cupboard, sweat starting to roll down his pudgy face. Ill have to check the other pipes. Yknow, the toilets and showers n stuff like that. Standing up, the overweight plumber cracked his back, and then removed a pen and pad from his back pocket, jotting down notes.

Garrison Turner ran his hands through his thinning hair. Donald wasnt one to miss details, and if he couldnt fix the flooding from their sinks and toilets, no other plumber could.

Unfortunately, thisll have to wait till tomorrow. I got a job oer at the McKenzie residence down the street. Seems the sewage drains runnin underneath their house broke down, and it came up through their sinks. He grimaced. Now its everywhere, on the walls and ceilings, and it aint pretty. He jotted down some reminder on his planner, and handed Mr. Turner a receipt.

This should be the cost of the visit, today an tomorrow, assuming I can get it fixed. Sorry bout the flooding.

Water under the bridge! Mrs. Turner walked into the kitchen, smiling as always. Youre welcome back at any time, were very eager to get this problem done with.

Thank ye very much, maam, but I should be off. Have a nice day, now. He nodded towards both of them and went out to his truck.

Shame. I guess well have to put up with it until hes back. Garrison turned to retire to his office, before yelping suddenly. Dag-nabbit!

Whats wrong, Garry? Harriet walked briskely over to him, hand on his back.

These new dentures, is what! Third time I bit myself this morning. He rubbed his jaw fervently. My old ones fit better.

Well, that just goes to show you have to be careful where you place things like that. Youre always losing things. She glanced over at the calendar hanging on the wall. Oh, you do remember you have that doctors appointment?

Almost forgot, Im going to be late! Loss of teeth and memory, he retorted. Snatching up his coat, he ran out the door. Harriet Turner walked out to the lawn, waving to him as he left.



And how are you feeling today, Mr. Turner? Dr. Hutchinson, flipping through his files, sat across the room looking confident.

Garrison shifted uncomfortabely, breathing steadily. Strangest thing. You know those stomach aches Ive been having? They just vanished! I havent taken anything for it, either. I mean, one night I feel like Ive swallowed a baseball, and the next I feel great. The doctor looked up oddly from his chair.

About when did the pain go away? He shifted through his notes, eyeing them carefully.

Oh, Id say around two days ago. The ironic thing is, now that I wont have to buy medicine, the pipes back up and I have to pay for a plumber. You win some, you lose some. Dr. Hutchinson looked up from his report, eyes narrowing.

Now, you saw youve had some problems with canker sores?

He rubbed his jaw some more, wincing from the sharp yet subtle pain. Correct. Its these new dentures that Ive bought. Theyre so big, I keep biting myself. Couldnt seem to find my old ones I lost a few days ago.

Lost? He went back to his notes.

I went to bed a couple nights ago, woke up, and they were gone. I swear I put them away, but even Harriet doesnt believe me. He sighed. Well, I suppose I could have fallen asleep with them still in my mouth. But that doesnt explain why theyve gone missing. The doctors eyes suddenly widened.

Wait, when did you say youre stomach pains went away?



Back at the house, Donald was grinning triumphantly.

Thank you for coming so soon! Mrs. Turner walked into the master bathroom, just adjacent from her bedroom.

Not a problem, maam. I think I found what was backing your pipes up. An object was blockin the drains that lead from this toilet right here. Lucky to have found it before anything serious happened.

Harriet looked over at the mangled object the plumber now held in his greasy hands. What is it? She asked.

Looks likea pair of dentures.

#11932 1 2006-10-26T03:35:44Z capt_weasle
The movie Most is a sad tale about a father and his son. I have not seen the movie, but I'll try to retell it as best I can. It's about a man who works a bridge, letting it up so that boats can pass under. One day, he takes his young son to work with him. As the day goes by, work passes slowly. Eventually, a boat comes to pass under the bridge. The man throws the switch, and the bridge rises. But he soon realizes that a train is coming, so he knows he must lower the bridge in order for it to pass over. As he goes to grab the lever, he notices his son is missing. After looking frantically, he finds that his son is stuck in the gears of the bridge. Now he must come to a decision. He must either pull the switch, letting the train pass by, saving a few hundreds of lives, or save his son, and let the train crash, possibly killing all of the passengers. Here is where you come in.

Which choice would you make? Would your save you son, or would you save the lives of the passengers? (You cannot save both)

For me, it would be a tough decision. Of course, I am not a father, so I couldn't really understand the scenario to its fullest. But if I place someone elses there, such as my sister, or even my parents (odd thought, though) it would be extremely difficult to bring myslef to let them die. I realize that I would be saving others, despite the fact that they are total stangers, but I couldn't live with myself if I knew I was killing someone I loved. Yet, based on my family beliefs, the person stuck in the gears would want me to save the people on the train, but of course I have no way of knowing that for sure. So, after thinking about it for a while, I would have to save the lives of the people on the train. I would never feel the same again, though.

Another question:
If someone implanted a bomb in your child and said that he'd detonate it in one month if you didn't kill 100 people. Assume that he'd detonate it prematurely if you tried to remove or deactivate the bomb, try yourself or have anyone try to get the remote, or rely on anyone other than yourself to resolve this situation (which includes calling the police). Also assume that you can trust the guy to remove the bomb if you do kill 100 people in 1 month.
Would you kill 100 people to save your son?

Ah, this is the exact reason I wish to buy the book "Saint", by Ted Dekker. It's pretty much that concept, except that it's a man and his wife, and the wife dies if the man does not kill some "wanted" men on the baddies to-do list.

And I'd have to go with saving the 100 people. Although that would be the hardest thing to do, killing my son. And that raises yet another question, but completely irrelevant (kind of). #12643 1 2006-12-05T04:45:32Z capt_weasle
Risk - The Game of Global Domination

For those of you who know the game, I welcome you. For those who do not, I advise you to stay away, unless you feel you can handle it and not be whiny. So, to start, I'd like to see who is interested in starting a good 'ole game of Risk. Now, before you attack me with a bunch of crap stuff, let me explain. First off, this is just to see who is interested for now. Next...

Why?
Well, I was thinking that it would be fun for us to get together and play some classic board games. Besides, the moderators are talking about staring with some new (ish) ideas, with some fresh spirits, and "carving your own legacy." And I say, heck, lets give it to them!

Wouldn't the game take way too long?
Yes, yes it would. But for starters it would make known the real time it takes to carry on a war, but that's a crappy reason. It would be a fun project to tackle, and it would give us something to do besides spamming up some "Count to A million!" game.

But that would be boring after a while
Yes, at some times, it would. But hey, everything takes time, right? (Yes I know that's no excuse)

So, you going to abandon this project like all your others?
I certainly hope not. That is why I want to enlist some "moderators" of the game to help keep it going. I would like an actual moderator or admin to help so they can update the stats when I am not around. Also some others to help do some other activities (will clarify later).

Wouldn't this be easier to do all at one time, on some downloadable internet version?
Unfortunately, yes. But not everyone has the time or resources to do so.

What about chances? Like dice rolls and cards?
You guys will have to trust me and the other moderators of the game to do this. You probably don't have any grounds to trust us, but you just have to. I am looking up some dice rollers on the 'net, but if that does not work I will resort to the die I have at my house (once again, trust is the issue). Same with the game cards.

So, how are you going to keep this up?
I will explain more later, but for now here is the deal: Either myself or the other mods will be rolling the dice, depending on who gets here first. Sadly, you have to roll the die according to the player, and he or she must respond to each roll before the next (i.e. whether or not they wish to retreat if they are on offense). This is where I would like to address another issue. We need players who will not abandon this game! I don't want people signing up and then not being around. This will take time, but I don't want this to die out! That also goes with rules. I don't want to take the time to explain things to you, unless it is a changed set of rules to make up for the fact we are using a forum. Look at the Wikipedia link at the top to learn the basics. If you sign up and don't know everything, it is up to you to find out. Any other questions will be asked in a discussion thread.

How many people can play?
Anywhere between 2-6 players at once.

What are the game types? (i.e. - Capitals, 100% Domination, <80% Domination, etc).
That will be decided once everything is sorted. Most likely it will be 100% (Global) Domination, because that is the most familiar.

Stats?
Once again, moderators will help keep those up in a Statistics Thread

What else?
Anything that needs to be addressed will be as we create our roles.

Oh, what board will we be using and how will it be kept?
I have spent the last while Gimp-ing (???) a board based on the computer version of the game, Risk II. There are some new territories, but through my experiences these make for better (and more fun) game play. The territories can be debated. The board can be found here. More on this later.


- - - - - - - - -

Well, I spent a bit of time thinking about this, and would love to here your opinions. Of course, I will be terribly busy, and am looking for a few trustworthy folks to devote their time and effort into making this an awesome experience. I am eagerly hoping that a moderator or administrator will be available to help out with this to make things go smoother.

So, who's in?

#273287 47 2006-12-19T06:43:08Z capt_weasle
Even if it were possible, would be really even use it? With the "messing up time" thing and all. Think about it.

Say you wanted, for whatever reason, to eliminate Person B. So you create a time travel device to destroy him at an early age. You go back in time successfully, and kill him. But there's a problem with that. If you kill Person B, you never meet him while you yourself are living, therefore the motivation to eliminate Person B is non-existent. So if you don't meet B, you don't want to kill B, you don't have any reason to go back in time. So, then what? You don't make a time machine.

There's my two cents.

#13127 1 2006-12-24T07:45:01Z capt_weasle
Well, compiled within the last few hours, I tried my best to do something worthwhile, somewhat anyway. I know it is a bit early, but for some people it is Christmas Eve, and I won't be here later in the night to post this before Christmas my time. So, here is my lame attempt at being poetic in....

The Night Before Christmas - Metanet Style!

'Twas the night before Christmas, and all through IRC, not a member was speaking, except of course me.

The computers were left on the desks in their rooms, and the chatting did not ever seem to resume.

Each person was nestled, all snug in their chairs, not concerned with the time or the state of affairs.

With KA in his kerchief, and LV in his cap, they had just left the chat room for a long winters nap.

When out on the desktop there arose such a clatter, The Blizz sprang from his post to see what was the matter.

Out of the net he scrolled with a flash, straight to the front screen with an incredible crash.

The icons laid out with a brilliant glow, gave the luster of objects appearing below.

When, what to his wandering eyes should be shown, but a miniature sleigh pulled by a drone.

With a thin little driver, who could be seen now and then, I knew in a moment it must be St. N.

More rapid than ninjas his coursers they came, and he whistled and shouted and called them by name.

Now Stupid, now Seeker, now Laser and Chain Gun! On Floor Guard, on Thwumper, on Hal and on Reggie!

To the side of the screen, and through the glass wall! Now seek away! Seek away! Seek away all!

As members that flee before Suki tirades, when met with an obstacle, through the sky they parade.

So to the desktop his coursers they flew, with a sled full of games, carrying St. N, too.

As Sweep awoke in his bed, and was looking around, out came St. N, with a noisily bound.

He had on not that much, a mere hat on his head, he was a thin figure, all colored in red.

A pack full of games, he flung on his back, and he looked like a peddler, just opening his pack.

His eyes couldnt be seen, but I knew he was merry; his cheeks were like roses, his color bright cherry.

He stood with a posture that made him look bold, his name on his hat, etched in pure gold.

In his left hand, he carried mines for the naughty, the spoiled brats, and those who were haughty.

An fr34king 1337 ninja, he was sleek and quite thin; I stood when I saw him, a devout follower of N.

With a nod of his head, and a shift of his feet, he went straight to work, to continue his feat.

He filled the stockings, each one with a game, and then he turned back around, and called out a name.

He dashed to his sleigh, let out a sharp cry, and jumped off the screen, and away he did fly.

But I heard him exclaim, as he drove out of sight,

Merry Christmas to all, and to all a good night!

#15260 1 2007-05-17T02:34:30Z capt_weasle
This is all preparation for the actual game. Everyone is allowed in, but make sure you will have time to participate in this. It will still take a while to put together, so it may not begin until summer. But to get things set up, here is the first assignment...

- You are an eager politician from Earth, wanting to get your hands on land in order to start your own government.

First Assignment Land Grab![/b]
- Using pictures, highlight which area of Colossi you would like to receive. The limit is one country.
- To support your claims, you must write a one paragraph essay explaining why you would like to receive what you are asking for. This is a formal letter to the founder, Jacob Colossi, and he will take into account spelling, grammar (it doesnt have to be perfect), and most importantly, valid reasons to support your argument.
- If someone else has already asked for something you want, go ahead and cut up the country to your personal desires (you still have to leave some portion to the other person). Remember, you are competing for territory! If you choose this path, your essay will be graded more severely, seeing how you are taking away someone elses land! Another reason to make sure your essay is well thought-out: after the other player has made a counter-argument (see point below), you do not get a chance for rebuttal!
- If you have chosen territory, and someone else has submitted a request to take that land away from you (see the previous point), you may write one counter-argument to explain why that person is being unfair or idiotic, and why you want your land back. If you have a valid, decent, and supported basis, the country may be divided more equally among the two, but the final decision will be made by Colossi and his fellow delegates.
- All of this is important! Even if you do not get what you want, save your anger. There will be time in the game to settle these disputes via some kind of treaty between the two countries, or even open battle, with the victor deciding the outcome of the borders (more will be explained later).
- After the deadline has passed, a final map will be drawn out to determine the final countries and their borders.


Map of Colossi. Countries are numbered.


Example:

One member wants this portion of land. They highlight it in red.


A second member decides they want this country, so they highlight it in blue (leaving some space for the other member - see rules!)


The first member wants their land back, and rebuttals. This is how it will look now:


After some talking amongst the main leaders of Colossi, this is how the final borders are drawn out:



Secondary Rules:
- So, if you notice, things can get crowded. If you want some land in a certain country, but it is full, please consider going elsewhere.
- Don't be too greedy.
- The total limit of players is 20-30 (pending)

Some Advice...
- Choose realistic names for countries and people. Try out a name website or something to come up with unique yet stylish names.
- Think about what government you set up. This will have a major affect on later game portions. Just as an example, if you set up a democracy, your country will be eligible to elect new leaders every now and then, and that means you will not be able to play as leader unless you are re-elected! Have caution. This will all be explained later.
- - - - - - - -

So, if there are any questions, PM me. Post your 'letters' and and pictures depicting your desired land here. Good luck and have fun. Deadline is ONE MONTH.

#389564 59 2007-12-15T06:45:31Z capt_weasle
Eh, not quite. It's hard to wrap around, but I think that it isn't exactly "set in stone." I have either cookies or brownies. I decide I want the brownies, but I have the will to change my mind and eat the cookies instead. Of course no matter what happens in the end, that exact choice is what happens. Because we have the choice to... choose and make those decisions, we have free will. I do something because it's something I choose to do. I don't do something because something is controlling me and telling me to take the cookie and not the brownie. That's my best. I'm terrible at translating thoughts to word.

#390597 59 2007-12-18T07:05:17Z capt_weasle
Quote: blue_tetris, December 16, 2007 09:34 am
Lastly, to capt_weasle: An atheistic determinist sees the world as entirely governed by the interactions of atoms, forces, and natural laws. The mind isn't anything special; it's just a funny word for "brain". It's a lump of meat. It is full of axioms and dendrites, which fire synapses and respond to neurotransmitters. There are similar systems at work in the water cycle and in computers. Do these things have free will?

If a hammer is dropped in the air, there's nothing random to its motion. A physicist could calculate its path for you. A single cause ends with a single effect. We see this kind of predictability in our world every day, and it allows us to do engineering and make technological advances. If a single cause results in a single event, why should a single jolt of electricity across the surface of a human organ result in two possible outcomes? What makes the atoms in the brain different than the atoms in an hammer? Why does the electricity in one lump of matter decide where to go, while the electricity traveling through another would be utterly derpived of choice?

Why should an atheist view his or her organs as somehow exempt from the perfectly mathematical laws of nature? The earth doesn't decide to rotate, my refrigerator doesn't decide to cool my foods, my spleen doesn't decide to filter waste from my bloodstream, my brain doesn't decide to keep me home from work some Thursdays. These things happen because external and internal properties have resulted in specific outcomes. If I believe in a world where my brainmeat's atoms get freewill, it's perfectly logical for me to believe in a world where sofa atoms and umbrella atoms get freewill also.

Yet through that definition we should all be rocks? Because human does not equal hammer, does not equal umbrella, does not equal whatever. You can say that the brain fires some stuff here, reacts through stuff here, and eventually comes up with the best mathematical pathway to best lead you from the couch to the toilet. Your brain gets the impulse from your eyes that say "idiot, there is a foot stool there. Walk around," and the brain calculates, and you eventually move. You can make every logical statement to determine that our brains work scientifically, and that situation A results in situation B; no more, no less. But that is all cut down with the one thing that really defines some line between us and a hammer: a difference between right and wrong. Two people are drowning, your wife and your child. Your brain calculates that the most logical thing to do is save your wife, as you can only save one. However, you and your wife both know that saving your child is better, reasons you can decide. You go against every logical instinct in your body to save your child. Why? Because that is what you think is right. I think this is essentially our "free will," to determine what makes things moral, and what does not. If we only worked on logic, we'd be in a terrible world. In terms of making things work it would be a perfect world, as if logic prevails then everything will eventually be scientifically designed to be perfect. But in such a place, there would probably be - just as an example - the worst form of slavery. We recently discussed Time on the Cross, a book which showed how economically profitable slavery actually was. Take away what John C. Calhoun said about slavery (his reasons were through racism, not economics), and slavery was a system that turned out a major profit. I wish I had the introduction to Time still, but it seemed to show slavery worked. Yet take one step back and slavery is obviously horrific, proven through American history, the Holocaust (Jewish in forced labor camps), etc. If you think otherwise, you are a rock. When the debate of God vs. Science boils down, I think the major factor is what is right vs. what is correct.

#18491 1 2007-12-20T23:23:56Z capt_weasle
So, I know this shouldn't be going live until the 24th, but I won't be here when that rolls 'round, so this year y'all get yer present a little early. Anyway, I spent quite a few hours making this last year, and I suppose I can make it a yearly posting (like my Christmas sig for you who remember). With further adieu, I give you...

The Night Before Christmas - Metanet Style!

'Twas the night before Christmas, and all through IRC, not a member was speaking, except of course me.

The computers were left on the desks in their rooms, and the chatting did not ever seem to resume.

Each person was nestled, all snug in their chairs, not concerned with the time or the state of affairs.

With KA in his kerchief, and LV in his cap, they had just left the chat room for a long winters nap.

When out on the desktop there arose such a clatter, The Blizz sprang from his post to see what was the matter.

Out of the net he scrolled with a flash, straight to the front screen with an incredible crash.

The icons laid out with a brilliant glow, gave the luster of objects appearing below.

When, what to his wandering eyes should be shown, but a miniature sleigh pulled by a drone.

With a thin little driver, who could be seen now and then, I knew in a moment it must be St. N.

More rapid than ninjas his coursers they came, and he whistled and shouted and called them by name.

Now Stupid, now Seeker, now Laser and Chain Gun! On Floor Guard, on Thwumper, on Hal and on Reggie!

To the side of the screen, and through the glass wall! Now seek away! Seek away! Seek away all!

As members that flee before Suki tirades, when met with an obstacle, through the sky they parade.

So to the desktop his coursers they flew, with a sled full of games, carrying St. N, too.

As Sweep awoke in his bed, and was looking around, out came St. N, with a noisily bound.

He had on not that much, a mere hat on his head, he was a thin figure, all colored in red.

A pack full of games, he flung on his back, and he looked like a peddler, just opening his pack.

His eyes couldnt be seen, but I knew he was merry; his cheeks were like roses, his color bright cherry.

He stood with a posture that made him look bold, his name on his hat, etched in pure gold.

In his left hand, he carried mines for the naughty, the spoiled brats, and those who were haughty.

An fr34king 1337 ninja, he was sleek and quite thin; I stood when I saw him, a devout follower of N.

With a nod of his head, and a shift of his feet, he went straight to work, to continue his feat.

He filled the stockings, each one with a game, and then he turned back around, and called out a name.

He dashed to his sleigh, let out a sharp cry, and jumped off the screen, and away he did fly.

But I heard him exclaim, as he drove out of sight,

Merry Christmas to all, and to all a good night!


p.s. - this is in general community because I made this for... well, the general community. Mods: move this if it suits your fancy.

#404162 29 2008-01-25T23:50:25Z capt_weasle
Quote: Keron Cyst, January 25, 2008 03:47 pm
Now that I think about it some more, Cloverfield shouldn't be compared to Godzilla at all; it's more like the latest War of the Worlds than anything else... an exponentially superior version of it.
Yeah, although credit to H.G. Wells for the book (books are better than movies, in general). But, to respond to Keron's post...

Spoiler: EGAD! Spoilers!The reason for the "minions" was probably based on the idea of earlier renderings of the monster, which was closer to a whale in appearance, and the things falling off it were like monster parasites attatched to the larger monster. When a "monster louse" bites a human, I suppose there is an immense reaction against the human body, which makes them... well... explode (poor girl). I'll agree with your points about scaling the building and surviving the crash (the pilot died, though! Then again, the pilot always dies). Especially the second camera fall after Hud gets partially eaten. The monster hesitating before the gobbling up Hud (my favorite character ) was likely due to the fact that the military was preparing a 'final' attack. And, of course, the camera survives that one, too. Must've been an expensive camera. Two more things: the very last scene (at Coney Island) there is an object falling from the sky into the water, which the director said was a satellite, though I don't know how that would contribute to the monster (awakening it, perhaps?). Also, the words after that scene are supposed to be from a radio transmission saying "It's still alive!" Just for those who were wondering....

Anyway, I agree that it was very similar to War of the Worlds (well, the 2nd one. Not so much the first). Still recommended though, unless you get motion sickness.

Edit: Oh, and it's Hud, Keron, Hud. Not Han.

A fun game I do on my computer when I'm tired of typing an essay? Type in a word, or select a previous word, right click, and go to synonyms. I keep replacing the original word with synonyms until, possibly, I arrive at an antonym to the original word. Now, I actually get bored of my boredom relief, so I'm not sure if this will eventually work. But, let's give it a shot, shall we?

Original word: Funny

Synonym: Humorous

Remember: I don't know if this will work, but hey, you're looking for boredom relief and gosh darn it here it is.

Hmm, interesting thought: Use your talents to interact with others. #20555 1 2008-06-17T22:43:18Z capt_weasle
You can't say that liberty and equality are the same thing. In a perfect world, the two would go hand-in-hand, but obviously we haven't reached that point. Alexis de Tocqueville observed that 19th century Americans generally were more fond of equality than freedom. I think this is true, especially today, because there naturally tends to be an inequality when everyone has absolute freedom. Face it, some people are just better at math, better at sports, better at whatever. So, what should be the higher priority today? Equality would ensure equal rights to everyone, without any discrimination whatsoever; however, it could put limits on freedom. Someone at my school was discussing this book (which the name I have forgotten) where, in the near future, everyone was equal, yet there were limits on those who were naturally more gifted - perhaps the better basketball player had to play with special glasses that limited vision whilst playing against another player with poor vision. Of course that isn't to say you couldn't increase the skills of the poor player instead of reducing the skills of the better one, but until we reach the point where equality and liberty touch, what should we put more emphasis on? Liberty, where I may do as I please? Or equality, where everyone has an equal opportunity to "succeed" in life, whatever that may be? Just an interesting topic brought up while reading Democracy in America.

#21006 1 2008-08-03T05:22:25Z capt_weasle
Article

First off, as to the topic title, that's what /she/ said.

Anyway, found that lovely little article after logging out of Yahoo mail. I figured it was just a writeup about the fuel efficiency of the Smart car, whatever. However, the overall tone of the whole piece made me want to puke. Here's a sample from the ending:

"When I pulled out of a downtown garage one afternoon, a young man paused to admire the car with his girlfriend. She tilted her head, looked at the Smart like it was a puppy, and smiled. As I drove off, she grabbed her boyfriend's hand, planted a kiss on his cheek, and they walked off arm-in-arm."

Really? Please. Look, it's a great car and all (despite a three star crash rating) but the whole "go green" fad is starting to get about as bad as Mac users. Mac = good computer (mostly for the arts), but the people who go nuts about it who won't shut up about how great it is? No thanks. I'm not really understanding this green thing. I mean yeah, it's a good cause, but - at least in America - there's been a terrible execution about it. Did anyone here ever see the commercial for that "Battleground Earth" show that's coming out? Yes! Another opportunity to cash in on fads like this. People have cared about the Earth forever, but now producers of shows like this just want to grab some quick money, and while there are certain great ideas to the show, I doubt the stars will deeply care for the cause they are supporting. It's publicity for them so everyone can faint over how "green" they are. It just annoys me.

For those of you who care about the Earth, great. I like the rock, too. Just please don't shove it in my face every time I go outside. Anyway, I'm awful at this ranting business, so you guys can jump in and throw in your two cents.
